Two `PostPass::Bloom` slots in the same stack are legal and double-apply; each invocation reuses the same pyramid and overwrites mip levels mid-frame — documented as user responsibility, not guarded against. `BloomParams` is added to `tungsten-core::post` and re-exported through `lib.rs`. `examples/04_shader_playground` gains the `KeyL` toggle, `Y/H U/J I/K` live-tune actions, an emissive-quad sprite for the LDR demo fixture, and the `TUNGSTEN_BLOOM_FIXTURE` + `TUNGSTEN_POST_STACK_FIXTURE=bloom_only` env pins. The smoke matrix gains a bloom row; the showcase capture and regen recipe land alongside `docs/showcase/`.

## D-061 — M29 2D forward normal-mapped lighting
**Date:** 2026-04-25
**Decision:** Ship 2D forward lighting as a sibling pipeline inside the scene pass. `Light { kind, color, intensity }` and a closed `LightKind::{ Point, Directional }` enum live in `tungsten-core::components`; `AmbientLight(Vec3)` is a world resource defaulting to `Vec3::ONE`. Render-side `LightingResources` owns one 544-byte `LightUbo` (16 lights × 32 bytes + `vec4<u32>` count_pad + `vec4<f32>` ambient) bound at group 2 of a new `LitSpritePipeline`; the pipeline reuses the existing sprite vertex/instance layout and binds a parallel albedo + normal + emissive bundle at group 1 (three texture views + one filtering sampler). Sprites carrying optional `normal_map` / `emissive_mask` siblings in the manifest pack into parallel atlas canvases keyed by the same packed rect — no second packer call, no separate sprite ids, no UV plumbing. `extract_sprites_default` flips `SpriteBatch.lit` on the resolved `SpriteAsset.lit_atlas.is_some()` axis; lit batches force `material_id = None` and warn when a sprite carries both (lit wins, M29 explicit non-goal). `extract_lights` runs every frame, culls by camera-AABB squared-distance, retains directionals first, caps at `LIGHT_CAP = 16`. The four lighting WGSL files (`lit_sprite`, `emissive_mask`, `rim_light`) live under `assets/shaders/` (lit) and `assets/shaders/stock/` (helpers), mirror byte-for-byte to `crates/tungsten-render/src/shaders/stock/`, register in the manifest, and pre-seed the `ShaderModuleCache` at ids `8..=10` (after sprite + SMAA + bloom). Body-edit hot reload routes through `Renderer::reload_shader` → `LitSpritePipeline::rebuild_with_shader` for the lit shader; the helpers are validated only (no pipeline behind them). Normal textures upload as linear `Rgba8Unorm` so tangent-space vectors are not gamma-decoded; emissive uploads premultiplied alpha-as-RGB so single-channel masks render consistently. With no lit sprites and an empty light list the captured frame stays byte-identical to the M28 baseline — the unlit pipeline is unchanged and the lit pipeline never runs.
**Why:** Sibling files share UV with the albedo for free, so the lit pool keys off the existing `TextureHandle` and the extract layer needs only one new boolean (lit) — strictly less code than parallel sprite ids would require, and it keeps `D-009`'s ID-only seam intact. The lit pipeline is a sibling rather than a `material_id` extension because lit shading is a renderer-owned global (per-frame UBO, not per-entity), so squeezing it into the per-material `UniformOverrideBlock` would force every material author into the lighting model. Forward shading with a 16-light cap fits 2D scenes (UBO size clamps at 544 bytes, comfortably under wgpu's 16 KiB minimum spec) and avoids dragging a deferred path into Phase 4. `Directional` lights always retained because losing them under heavy point-light pressure dims the entire scene noticeably, while losing the farthest point light is locally invisible. Tangent-space normals interpreted as world-axis is the conventional 2D shortcut: there is no per-fragment tangent frame for a sprite plane, so `(n.xy, n.z)` is read directly. Closed-enum `LightKind` mirrors `Easing` (`D-054` precedent): adding a spotlight is an explicit plan-file change, not a silent runtime registration. No new runtime dependency: `wgpu`, `bytemuck`, and `glam` already cover the surface.
**Consequences:** `SpriteAsset` grows optional sibling paths plus a `lit_atlas: Option<TextureHandle>` marker; `SpriteEntry` grows two optional sibling string fields; `extract_sprites_default` grows a `lit` keying axis (still byte-equal output when no sprite carries aux atlases). New shader id triple (`lit_sprite`, `emissive_mask`, `rim_light`) extends `D-053`'s body-edit reload table. Lit + material composition is intentionally out-of-scope: a sprite carrying both warns and uses lit; rotating a lit sprite is a documented M33 follow-up (the 2D shader does not rotate the sampled normal). Narrows neither `D-058` nor `D-060`: emissive bright pixels feed the existing M28 bloom thresholding without additional wiring; `SceneColor` stays sRGB. The platformer example gains four `walk_*_n.png` and four `walk_*_e.png` siblings, an `orbit_lights_system`, and a `TUNGSTEN_LIGHTING_FIXTURE=on|off` env switch that gates light spawn + lit-batch routing on the player. The smoke matrix gains a lighting row; the showcase capture (`docs/showcase/lighting_off_vs_on.png`) and regen recipe land alongside the M27/M28 entries.

## D-062 — Physics broadphase: flat prefix-sum spatial hash with drift-bounded restaging
**Superseded by D-075:** staging, drift-budget and pair-prefilter clauses only; the flat hash, exact-cell entries and serial determinism stand.
**Amended by D-080:** the hashed slot table with exact-cell entries is the fall-back layout; compact worlds get a direct cell table, and the safety-net sweep has a statics-only grid.
**Date:** 2026-07-07
**Decision:** Replace the `HashMap<IVec2, Vec<u32>>` uniform grid with a flat prefix-sum spatial hash inside `SpatialGrid` (same public `insert`/`query` API): `insert` stages `(id, cell range)` records, and the first query after staging builds the flat structure — count cell refs per slot, exclusive prefix sum, scatter ids into one flat entry array. Unbounded cell coordinates map into a power-of-two slot table via a multiplicative hash (Ericson RTCD constants + xor fold); each entry stores its exact cell so hash aliasing merges buckets without producing false candidates. The grid is staged once at frame start from pre-integration proxies — dynamic AABBs inflated symmetrically by one substep of travel (`|v|·sub_dt`) — and reused across substeps under a **drift budget**: each substep accumulates the max per-substep travel, and when the total exceeds the half-cell query margin the grid is restaged from current positions and the budget resets. Settled scenes therefore build once per frame; extreme-velocity scenes degrade gracefully to the old per-substep rebuild. Pair generation additionally prefilters grid candidates with an actual AABB overlap test (query AABB = swept + half-cell margin vs candidate swept AABB) before pushing a pair. This is step 1 of `docs/plans/archive/physics-scale-and-ccd.md`.
**Why:** At 3k–25k bodies the hashed grid paid SipHash on every cell touch and dropped every per-cell `Vec` allocation on `clear()`, up to 8× per frame (once per substep). Counting-sort flat grids are the canonical structure for dense uniform-size 2D workloads: zero steady-state allocations, cache-linear scatter and scan. Unfiltered pair lists were the second half of the cost: every candidate in an overlapped cell entered the pair list and was narrow-phase tested in all solver iterations; the AABB prefilter removes those pairs at broadphase cost. The drift budget exists because the two naive alternatives both fail at scale: full-frame travel inflation keeps one build per frame but fattens fast-body AABBs to ~12 cells each, multiplying per-cell density and query scans (measured +25–61% on 10k/25k projectile scenarios); per-substep inflation without restaging lets moving targets leave their staged cells, silently dropping dynamic-vs-dynamic pairs at moderate speeds. Bounding staleness by the query margin keeps candidate-set semantics equivalent to the per-substep rebuild while paying for restages only when bodies actually move far.
**Consequences:** Supersedes the "uniform spatial grid broad-phase rebuilt per substep, no persistent state" clause of `D-033` — the grid now persists across substeps within its drift budget (still no cross-frame proxy persistence). The rest of `D-033` stands: physics stays hand-rolled, no external crate. Proxies are still re-gathered per substep in deterministic `World` order, so grid ids remain valid frame-wide; steps 2–5 of the plan (contact persistence, speculative CCD with fixed substeps, island sleeping, SoA gather) will extend this entry's scope and are recorded separately when they land. Gauss–Seidel positional corrections are not counted against the drift budget (matching the old code, which also covered them only by the query margin); the GS-slip and corner-escape regression tests bound that exposure.

## D-063 — Physics solver: contact persistence + warm-started soft constraints
**Superseded by D-076:** per-substep impulse-map rebuild only; warm-start values, contact aging and solver order stand.
**Amended by D-092:** the substep order only; an arrival pass runs between position integration and the relax iteration. The contact buffer, the warm start and the solver stand.
**Date:** 2026-07-07
**Decision:** Replace the per-iteration MTV projection solver with a Box2D-v3-shaped soft step (step 2 of `docs/plans/archive/physics-scale-and-ccd.md`). Per substep: the narrow phase runs **once** into a contact buffer (pair key, normal, penetration, precomputed inverse effective mass, approach velocity); solver iterations then work on cached contacts with **accumulated normal impulses clamped ≥ 0**, **warm-started** from a pair-keyed impulse map that survives across substeps and frames (flat open-addressing table with multiplicative hashing — never `std::HashMap`, per `D-062`; entity proxies key by id+generation, tile proxies by a position hash; the map is rebuilt from live contacts each substep so stale pairs age out for free). Raw in-place MTV projection is replaced by a **soft constraint bias**: `PhysicsConfig` gains `contact_hertz` (30), `contact_damping_ratio` (10), `linear_slop` (0.25 px), `max_push_speed` (120 px/s), `restitution_threshold` (30 px/s); effective hertz is capped at a quarter of the substep rate for stability, and contacts against immovables use twice the hertz. The substep order becomes: contacts → gravity → warm start → biased velocity iterations (`solver_iterations`) → position integration (bias velocity becomes depenetration) → one bias-free relax iteration (strips injected bias energy) → restitution replaying the stored approach velocity above the inelastic threshold. Dynamic bodies without colliders integrate once per frame outside the solver. The speculative static sweep and collision-event semantics are unchanged: events emit from the pre-solve narrow-phase pass — the same contact set the old solver emitted on iteration 0 (documented in `resting_contact_keeps_emitting_collision_events`).
**Why:** The old solver re-ran the narrow phase in all 4 Gauss–Seidel iterations (the single largest cost at pile density) and its full-penetration MTV pushes re-injected energy every step: settled piles never calmed below ~97 px/s average speed (no sleep criterion could engage — step 4 is blocked on this), and under pile pressure in-place corrections shoved bodies past thin-wall centerlines, ejecting them out the far side (7 escapes in ~400 steps from a 3k pile in 80 px walls; one escaped body pins the global substep count at the cap ×8 forever). Warm-started accumulated impulses + soft bias + a low-speed inelastic restitution threshold are the standard fix (Catto's Solver2d, Box2D v3): measured post-step-2, the 10k settled-pile jitter floor drops 114 → 11.5 px/s, the 3k/80 px containment run leaks 0 bodies over 2,400 steps, and `dense_pile/3000` lands under the 4 ms budget.
**Consequences:** Positional resolution is no longer instantaneous: bodies rest at ~`linear_slop` penetration and deep overlap recovers at the bias rate (capped by `max_push_speed`) over several frames instead of one MTV push — position-precision tests assert convergence-to-slop rather than same-step resolution, and stacked columns sink slightly with load until step 3's fixed substeps raise the hertz cap (currently 15 Hz effective at 1 substep/60 FPS). Supersedes `D-062`'s "no cross-frame persistence" note for impulses only (proxies are still re-gathered per substep); `D-033`'s hand-rolled mandate stands, no new dependency. Contact-begin timing shifts by one substep at frame boundaries (contacts are measured pre-integration), which is invisible at 60 FPS but means a first-touch event can land one frame later than under the old post-integration narrow phase. Steps 3–5 (speculative CCD + fixed substeps, island sleeping, SoA gather) build directly on this contact buffer.

## D-064 — Physics CCD: speculative contacts + fixed substeps
**Amended by D-092:** velocity the solver injects after admission is checked against every neighbour it reaches, static, sleeping or dynamic; speculative contacts stay the primary CCD and the statics sweep stays the last net.
**Date:** 2026-07-08
**Decision:** Speculative contacts become the primary CCD and the substep count becomes fixed (step 3 of `docs/plans/archive/physics-scale-and-ccd.md`). The narrow phase turns signed-distance: `aabb_vs_aabb_speculative` / `circle_vs_circle_speculative` / `aabb_vs_circle_speculative` admit broadphase pairs separated by a positive gap as contacts with **negative penetration**, up to a per-pair margin of one substep of relative travel plus slack (`|v_a − v_b|·sub_dt + 4·linear_slop`). No solver change is needed: the existing separated branch (`s = linear_slop − penetration > 0`) already biases by `s/sub_dt`, which removes exactly the excess approach velocity so a fast body arrives at touching instead of passing through — this closes all four tunneling classes (static AABB, static circle, dynamic wall, head-on dynamic pairs) at every tested speed up to 15,360 px/s. Restitution survives because it replays the approach velocity stored at contact build (`D-063`), so an elastic bullet clamped to arrival still rebounds at full speed. The global velocity-derived substep heuristic (`compute_substeps`) is **deleted**: `PhysicsConfig` replaces `max_substeps` (8) with fixed `substeps` (4), and `solver_iterations` drops to 1 (substeps-over-iterations, the Box2D-v3 "TGS Soft" shape; the effective contact-hertz cap rises 15 → 60 Hz at 60 FPS). Ghost collisions on tile seams are mitigated in the speculative functions themselves: corner/vertex gap normals clamp to the exposed axis when the other involved face is internal per `face_mask`, and pure face gaps on internal faces drop. The slab sweep survives only as a statics-only safety net for solver-injected velocity a pair admission never saw (impulse chains mid-substep), now also covering static circles via conservative circle→AABB bounding-square promotion. Collision events gain an explicit gate: contacts emit only with `penetration > 0` — a speculative positive-gap contact stays silent until actual touch (pinned by `speculative_gap_contact_emits_no_event_until_touch`).
**Why:** The old speculative slab sweep covered exactly one pair class (fast dynamic vs static AABB); circle targets, dynamic walls, and head-on dynamic pairs tunneled 100% at ≥7,680 px/s because no swept path existed for them, and adding sweeps per class scales badly. Speculative contacts need only signed distance (trivial for AABB/circle without rotation) and cover every class through the same solver branch at near-zero extra cost; the per-pair velocity-scaled margin keeps settled piles from admitting useless gap contacts. The substep heuristic let one fast (or leaked) body multiply whole-world cost ×8 (`pile_plus_bullet` ran 3.7× its dense-pile base at 10k); with tunneling safety decoupled from substep count, a fixed count both kills that amplification and makes solver stiffness deterministic. Fixed 4×1+relax over 1×4+relax follows Catto's Solver2d results (`D-063` research digest).
**Consequences:** Supersedes `D-033`'s "velocity-derived substeps" clause (the hand-rolled mandate and the rest of `D-033` stand; `D-062`'s drift-budget staging is unchanged and now sees at most 4 substeps of drift). Settled scenes that ran 1 substep under the heuristic now run 4 — settled-pile cost rises (recorded in the plan's step-3 table) until step 4's island sleeping collapses steady-state work; fast scenes that pinned the old cap of 8 run 2× fewer substeps. `PhysicsConfig::max_substeps` is gone (API break, no external users); tests that need the old single-substep behavior set `substeps = 1` explicitly. Bodies clamped by a speculative contact come to rest at ~`linear_slop` penetration one substep earlier than a discrete contact would have; first-touch events land on the following substep when penetration is real. The safety-net sweep can clamp a diagonal pass across a static circle slightly early (bounding-square promotion) — acceptable for a last-resort net that fires only above one min-half-extent of travel per substep.

## D-065 — Physics island sleeping
**Amended by D-082:** the keyed sleep map and its per-frame rebuild only; sleep state sits in arrays parallel to the proxies and is rebuilt by entity key when the body sequence changes.
**Date:** 2026-07-08
**Decision:** Settled bodies sleep per island (step 4 of `docs/plans/archive/physics-scale-and-ccd.md`). A serial, deterministic union-find (min-index root, path halving) over each frame's **touching** contacts (`penetration > 0`, accumulated across substeps) groups awake dynamic bodies into islands; an island sleeps once every member's speed stays below `PhysicsConfig::sleep_threshold` (20 px/s default; `<= 0` disables sleeping) for `time_to_sleep` (0.5 s). Sleeping bodies stay staged in the broadphase but never initiate pair queries — sleeping–sleeping pairs skip the narrow phase entirely — and skip gravity, position integration, and writeback; velocities are zeroed at sleep, and an awake body's contact against a sleeper solves with effective inverse mass 0 (immovable, stiffer static softness). Sleep state is a side table in `PhysicsBuffers` (flat open-addressing map keyed by the warm-start entity key — no `std::HashMap`), never a component; the public surface is `physics::wake(world, entity)` plus `PhysicsBuffers::{wake, is_sleeping, sleeping_count}`. Wake paths: (1) a contact from an awake body — a touching contact with relative speed above the threshold, or a speculative gap contact approached faster than the threshold, so a bullet wakes its impact fringe before touch resolves (D-064's gap admission) while resting-speed neighbors stay frozen — wake waves damp out where motion dies instead of sweeping the pile, so a poke wakes only the local region; (2) an external `Position`/`Velocity` write, detected without ECS write-tracking because a sleeper's components are bit-frozen by the step (writeback skips sleepers), so any gather-time mismatch against the stored sleep state is an external write and wakes the island; (3) member despawn or component removal — the per-frame sleep-map rebuild drops entries with no live proxy and wakes their island; (4) the `wake` API, which wakes the whole island. An island falling asleep while resting on an already-sleeping island adopts that island's tag (bridging two sleeping islands merges their tags), so island wakes reach bodies that settled on top after the support slept. **Event semantics (Box2D precedent):** a resting contact keeps emitting `CollisionEvent`s every step while awake, a sleeping island emits nothing (its contacts never reach the narrow phase; awake-vs-sleeping touching contacts still emit), and waking resumes emission — pinned by `resting_contact_emits_until_asleep_and_resumes_on_wake`; `D-064`'s no-event-on-gap rule is unchanged.
**Why:** Sleeping is the decisive lever for settled scenes (`D-064` accepted a ~3× settled-pile regression against exactly this step): steady-state cost collapses to the awake fringe. Per-island rather than per-body sleep is mandatory — a per-body criterion lets a sleeping support freeze under a still-moving load. Per-body *contact* wake (motion-gated) rather than Box2D's whole-island wake is what keeps a 20k pile usable: waking all members on any poke would restore full-churn frame cost for ≥0.5 s per hit, and the done-when demands a local wake. The `D-063` soft solver makes the criterion meaningful: the raw sleep-disabled jitter floor at 10k is 3.5 px/s average with per-step maxima of ~4–6 px/s at true rest — comfortably under the 20 px/s threshold. The min-member rule means occasional single-body micro-pops (~80 px/s for a frame or two) hold the whole island awake another 0.5 s each, giving a 10k pile a few seconds of tail before full sleep — accepted; the alternative (freezing a still-moving member) is visibly wrong.
**Consequences:** Sleeping is on by default; sims that must never freeze set `sleep_threshold = 0` (the jitter-floor regression test does). A sleeping island is infinitely stiff from outside — new load rests on it without compressing it until motion exceeds the wake tolerance; containment under pile pressure is therefore *safer* asleep (0/3000 over 2,400 steps post-change, and the harness collapses from ~12 s to ~5 s wall time as the pile sleeps mid-run). Sub-threshold creep (sustained < 20 px/s) can move a body a few px per sleep window without waking neighbors — invisible at these scales. Bodies can sleep mid-air in zero-gravity worlds (drifting below threshold) — matches Box2D, harmless here. `resting_contact_keeps_emitting_collision_events` is superseded by the sleep-aware event test; game code that polls resting contacts indefinitely must wake the island or read positions instead. With settled scenes slept, the per-substep proxy gather becomes the dominant residual cost — step 5's SoA gather attacks it next. `D-033`'s hand-rolled mandate stands; no new dependency; union-find and pair ordering stay serial for determinism.

## D-066 — Physics SoA frame staging: gather and write back once per frame
**Superseded by D-075:** the same-event-order clause only; events remain per substep and deterministic in pair-list order.
**Date:** 2026-07-11
**Decision:** Body state is staged into the dense proxy array **once per frame**, not per substep (step 5 of `docs/plans/archive/physics-scale-and-ccd.md`). The gather is a columnar 4-way ECS query — `World::query2_opt2::<Collider, Position, RigidBody, Velocity>`, a new query shape with two required and two optional components whose column presence resolves once per archetype — so no per-entity `get::<RigidBody>()`/`get::<Velocity>()` random lookups remain. Tilemap proxies are likewise staged in the same single gather. Substeps run entirely on the dense arrays: zero `World` reads or writes inside the substep loop. Writeback happens once per frame after the last substep through the mutable twin `query2_opt2_mut`, which iterates the identical archetype/row order as the gather, so proxies zip positionally with query rows (asserted in debug); sleeping bodies still skip writeback, keeping `D-065`'s bit-frozen invariant and gather-time external-write detection intact (the gather's `position + offset` fp ops are unchanged). Collision events still emit from each substep's pre-solve narrow phase, accumulate in the frame buffer, and drain to the `EventQueue` once per frame in substep order — same events, same order, one resource access. The per-proxy sleep-flag side vector is gone: proxies persist across substeps, so `Proxy::sleeping` set at frame start (and flipped by mid-substep contact wakes, which still take effect within the same substep) carries the flag frame-wide. Grid `ProxyId`s stay valid because the proxy array is built once and never reordered within the frame.
**Why:** After `D-065`, a fully-slept 10k pile still cost 2.76 ms/step, almost entirely the ×4-per-frame proxy re-gather (with two per-entity random component lookups per body) plus per-substep writeback — pure ECS traffic on unchanging data. The old per-substep round-trip (write back, re-read the same bits next substep) existed only for historical reasons: nothing runs between substeps, so the round-trip is an identity. Staging once removes 3 of 4 gathers, 3 of 4 writebacks, and all per-entity random lookups; Box2D v3's layout work showed most of its >2× came from exactly this class of change before threading.
**Consequences:** `physics_step` reads component state once at frame start and commits once at frame end; a mid-frame external component write can no longer be observed mid-frame (nothing legitimate could do that — no game code runs between substeps). A dynamic collider body **without** a `Velocity` component now keeps solver-injected velocity across the frame's substeps instead of resetting each substep (still resets across frames; such bodies gate gravity on `has_velocity` as before) — behavior for this degenerate class is slightly more correct, no test pins the old shape. The ECS surface gains `query2_opt2`/`query2_opt2_mut` (columnar optional-component access; the mutable twin asserts distinct types and documents the order guarantee the physics zip relies on). Determinism is untouched: same gather order, same pair ordering, serial union-find and solver (`D-033`/`D-062`–`D-065` stand; no new dependency, no `std::HashMap`, no threads). Step 6 (parallel solver via contact-graph coloring) remains conditional on the 25k-awake numbers.

## D-067 — Physics step stays serial: color-parallel solver evaluated and dropped
**Date:** 2026-07-12
**Decision:** The physics step remains single-threaded end to end. Step 6 of `docs/plans/archive/physics-scale-and-ccd.md` (scoped-thread parallel contact solver via greedy contact-graph coloring) was **implemented in full and dropped on measurement**. The build was the Box2D-v3 shape: pair collection greedily colored each pair with the lowest color free on both movable sides (statics and build-time-frozen sleepers don't constrain colors — with impulse application guarded on inverse mass > 0 they are read-only to the solver), pairs counting-sorted by color into a color-grouped contact buffer, and the contact passes (warm start, biased iterations, bias-free relax, restitution) plus gravity/position integration ran chunked across `std::thread::scope` workers with a sense-reversing spin barrier per color; narrow phase, wake path, union-find, events, and the speculative safety net stayed serial. Determinism held bit-exactly across runs and across every worker count (verified by state-hash tests at 1/2/4/8/auto workers; disjoint movable bodies per color make chunk scheduling unobservable). It was still dropped because `perf` attribution of the 25k-awake frame (settled, sleep-disabled, Ryzen 5 6600H) shows the premise was wrong: the contact-solve passes are ~1.7% of cycles — the frame is `SpatialGrid::query` (~33%) plus serial narrow phase/contact build (~61%, of which impulse-map probing ~12% and the AABB overlap prefilter ~4%). Measured wall times: 134.6 ms serial → 131.2 ms best (4 workers), 150 ms at 12, 303 ms at 16 (oversubscription); the coloring + counting-sort machinery alone regressed the serial 10k settled-awake state ~18% (36.1 → 42.8 ms). All step-6 code was reverted; what remains is the determinism gate `tests/physics_determinism.rs` (two full 3k-churn runs must produce identical position/velocity hashes).
**Why:** Amdahl closes the case: with the solve at ~2% of the frame, perfect scaling on 6 cores buys < 2 ms of 134.6 while the machinery costs more than that in serial overhead and code complexity (unsafe raw-pointer views, barriers, a threading-policy exception to the audio/watcher-only rule). The plan's step 6 was explicitly conditional — "dropping it is a valid outcome — record the numbers." The profile also names the real levers for the still-open ≤ ~16 ms 25k-awake goal: the ×4-per-frame pair query + narrow phase (the same pairs re-queried every substep from a grid staged at most once per frame — pair-list reuse across substeps is the algorithmic follow-up), the warm-start impulse-map probe pattern, and only then a parallel split of pair collection + narrow phase (safe deterministic splits exist — per-worker pair/contact runs concatenated in worker order — but wake ordering semantics need their own design).
**Consequences:** `AGENTS.md`'s threading policy is unchanged — the only background threads remain the `cpal` audio callback and the `notify` watcher; the physics step spawns no threads. `PhysicsConfig` gains no `solver_threads` field. Determinism stays trivially guaranteed and is now pinned by `tests/physics_determinism.rs` (any future parallelism must keep that test green bit-for-bit). The plan-level "25k awake churn ≤ ~16 ms" done-when stays unmet at 124.9 ms (`D-066` numbers stand); reaching it is future work along the levers above, not part of this plan's close-out. `D-033`/`D-062`–`D-066` stand.

## D-068 — Agent instructions: one shared body, scoped files, shared skills
**Amended by D-106:** the index budget only; `docs/LLM_INDEX.md` may hold 12 KiB; the other budgets and the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-09-25
**Decision:** `AGENTS.md` is the single instruction body for every coding agent; `CLAUDE.md` contains only `@AGENTS.md`. Scoped rules sit beside the code they govern (`crates/tungsten-render/AGENTS.md` with its own one-line `CLAUDE.md`). `docs/LLM_INDEX.md` is on-demand navigation rather than a mandatory startup read, and plan conventions live in `docs/plans/README.md`. The two project skills are tracked under `.claude/skills/` and exposed to Codex through `.agents/skills/<name>` symlinks. `.ignore` carries search hygiene; `.claudeignore` is retired because Claude Code 2.1.110 did not honor it. `scripts/check-agent-context.py` (`just ctx`) enforces the budgets (root `AGENTS.md` ≤ 6 KiB and under 200 lines, render file ≤ 4 KiB, index ≤ 8 KiB, skill description ≤ 300 characters, skill body ≤ 8 KiB) and checks links and symlinks.
**Why:** Claude Code and Codex now read the same text once (verified per client in `docs/agent-setup.md`), mandatory startup instruction files shrank from about 34.7 KB to about 6 KB of repo bytes, and renderer rules load only where they apply. The budgets are project limits that keep startup reads small, not client limits.
**Consequences:** Launch Claude Code from the repo root; a launch inside a subdirectory needs the one-time external-import approval before it sees the root rules. Add a scoped `AGENTS.md` only where a directory has unique rules. Client setup, permissions and search filters are documented in `docs/agent-setup.md`.

## D-069 — Pinned toolchain, edition 2024 and shared command gates
**Amended by D-103:** the compiler-bump clause only; from 1.0, a bump that raises `rust-version` lands in a minor release with a CHANGELOG line, never in a patch release; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-09-25
**Decision:** `rust-toolchain.toml` pins Rust 1.98.1 (minimal profile plus rustfmt and clippy), and the workspace `rust-version = "1.98.1"` declares that tested compiler as the support floor for all seven packages. The workspace uses edition 2024 and resolver 3; examples inherit the edition and `rustfmt.toml` sets `style_edition = "2024"` for direct rustfmt calls. `just` recipes wrap the raw cargo commands, and `just check` (format check, `clippy -D warnings`, all tests) replaces advisory clippy. `cargo-deny` (`deny.toml`) gates advisories, licenses (MPL-2.0 for symphonia and CC0-1.0 for notify are allowed explicitly) and sources (`unknown-git = "deny"`, no git sources).
**Why:** Reproducible local and CI results need one compiler; a lower support floor would need its own testing. Strict lint became possible once every warning was cleared. The dependency gate surfaced six baseline advisories that local builds never showed; the 2026-09 refresh cleared five.
**Consequences:** A compiler bump edits both pins and reruns the CPU and GPU gates plus a perf comparison. New lints are fixed, or narrowly `#[expect]`ed with a reason, never allowed workspace-wide. Each advisory exception in `deny.toml` names its ID and reason. Perf captures build with `TUNGSTEN_PERF_RUSTFLAGS` (default: frame pointers, generic CPU), and each capture records its compiler and flags.

## D-070 — CPU-only CI that reports, not blocks
**Amended by D-112:** the `just bench-build` step only; CI builds the benchmarks with `cargo bench --workspace --no-run --profile dev --locked`; the rest stands.
**Amended by D-121:** the job list only; a `windows-tests` job also runs `cargo test --workspace --locked` on `windows-2025`, reporting like the rest; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-09-25
**Decision:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the shared CPU gates on pull requests, manual dispatch and pushes to `main` and `0.*`: `just check`, `just bench-build`, `just deps`, `just ctx` and `just script-test`. Actions are pinned to reviewed commit SHAs and tools to checked releases (ShellCheck by SHA-256). The token is read-only, superseded runs are cancelled, and builds use generic `RUSTFLAGS="-C target-cpu=x86-64"`. GPU, audio, visual and perf checks stay local on the reference machine. No branch protection, required status checks or PR process is added.
**Why:** These checks are deterministic and cheap, and a clean checkout catches toolchain and dependency drift that a warm local tree hides. Hardware checks can't run on shared runners, and timings from them aren't comparable.
**Consequences:** Narrows `D-002`'s "no CI gate": CI informs and doesn't block merges, and local builds on the reference machine remain the bar for GPU, audio and perf. The Linux package list starts at pkg-config, ALSA and udev; the first clean run confirms it.

## D-071 — Tag-triggered release builds; the changelog cut sets the version
**Status:** Superseded by D-074 for README status coupling and release handoff, and by D-079 for the tag-push-only trigger; other clauses remain in effect (CPU variants amended by D-072).
**Amended by D-120:** the `CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_STRIP=debuginfo` and build-only clauses only; release binaries keep their line tables, split into a separate debug archive per target, and the run probes a crash report on the CPU; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-122:** the archive contents and the license-notices consequence only; archives also carry `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES-rust-std.html`, written by `scripts/release.py` from license data the build jobs collect; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-09-25
**Decision:** `.github/workflows/release.yml` runs only on pushed `v*` tags. A verify job runs `scripts/release.py check <tag>`. Build jobs compile the examples in the release profile for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` (pinned `ubuntu-24.04`) and `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` (static CRT), with generic `-C target-cpu=x86-64` and `CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_STRIP=debuginfo`. A publish job packages one archive per target, `SHA256SUMS`, and runs `gh release create` with the tag's `CHANGELOG.md` section as notes. Archives mirror the repo root (binaries, `tungsten.json`, `input.json`, `assets/`, each `examples/*/assets/`, `LICENSE`, `README.txt`) because examples read those paths relative to the working directory. Only the publish job gets `contents: write`; actions stay SHA-pinned. A tag whose version has a `CHANGELOG.md` section must equal the workspace version; a pre-release tag (`vX.Y.Z-<pre>`) without one is a rehearsal: the tree must still agree, the tag isn't compared with the version and `[Unreleased]` supplies the notes. Tags with a pre-release part are published as GitHub pre-releases. The workspace version always equals the newest `CHANGELOG.md` release heading and the README/DESIGN "Workspace `vX.Y.Z`" status lines, and changes only at the cut (`just release-cut X.Y.Z` moves `[Unreleased]`, bumps them and refreshes `Cargo.lock`). `just repo-check` and a first `ci.yml` step enforce it. The `tungsten-finalize` skill is the pre-finalize docs pass.
**Why:** Players and testers need binaries without a Rust toolchain, and Windows binaries need a Windows runner. A pushed tag is an explicit local decision to release, so `D-002`'s judgment-first process holds. Hosted runners have no GPU, so the pipeline builds and never launches. The consistency check fails in seconds, before the long builds. `gh` is preinstalled on runners, so no third-party release action is needed. Stripping debug info leaves codegen identical to the tuned profile (`D-041`).
**Consequences:** Extends `D-070` without superseding it: `ci.yml` stays read-only and informational, and GPU, audio and visual checks of released binaries stay manual on real hardware. Bumping the version at branch start fails `just repo-check`; bump at the cut. New examples come from the workspace `members` list; a new runtime file outside `assets/` goes into `SHARED_RUNTIME` in `scripts/release.py`. Archives don't yet bundle third-party license notices for statically linked crates. Linux binaries may need a glibc up to the ubuntu-24.04 image's (2.39).

## D-072 — Release archives ship x86-64-v3 and portable builds behind a launcher
**Amended by D-122:** the archive-root layout only; the root also holds the two third-party notices files; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-09-25
**Decision:** Release archives carry every example built at two CPU levels under `bin/<level>/`: `x86-64-v3` (AVX2, FMA, BMI1/2, F16C, LZCNT, MOVBE) and the portable `x86-64` baseline. At the archive root, each example name is a copy of `tungsten-launcher` (`tools/launcher`, standard library only, built at the baseline). The launcher runs the fastest shipped level whose features `is_x86_feature_detected!` reports, names it on stderr, and runs it with the archive root as the working directory, so an example starts from any folder or through a symlink. `TUNGSTEN_CPU_LEVEL` forces a shipped level. `release.yml` builds each `CPU_LEVELS` entry into `target/<level>`; `scripts/release.py package` requires the baseline and the launcher, and its `LEVELS` list must match the launcher's (tested). The tuned release profile is unchanged.
**Why:** Measured on the reference machine (Ryzen 5 6600H, rustc 1.98.1) against the portable build, with a baseline rerun as the drift control (criterion geomean −0.1%, frames within 3.6%). Whole-frame telemetry, 3 captures per row with NoMachine idle: `physics-stress` 3k −4.5% average and −4.6% p95 frame time with v3, 10k −4.4% and −5.1%; `ecs-high-load` +0.8% and +0.7%. Criterion: physics steps −3 to −8% and the broadphase rebuild −24% at every higher level, but spawn/despawn +3 to +31% and per-entity lookups +13 to +54%. `x86-64-v2` had the worst regressions. `native` and fat LTO matched v3 within noise, so neither a build-from-source helper nor fat LTO is adopted. A v3-only archive would crash with an illegal instruction on CPUs without AVX2; the launcher gives v3 speed where it's safe. Evidence: `perf-runs/cpu-levels-20260925/` (local).
**Consequences:** Supersedes `D-071`'s single `-C target-cpu=x86-64` build; the rest of `D-071` stands. Archives roughly double in size and CI builds each target twice. ECS- or spawn-heavy scenes can run about 1% slower on v3 CPUs; `TUNGSTEN_CPU_LEVEL=x86-64` is the escape hatch. Gains were measured on one Zen 3+ CPU only. The launcher knows `x86-64-v4` (AVX-512), but it isn't built because the reference machine can't measure or run it. A new level needs a measurement and a `CPU_LEVELS` entry. The fallback on a CPU without AVX2 is covered by unit tests and a missing-level run, not by real older hardware.

## D-073 — M30 game feel: CPU parallax at extract, trauma over the sine carrier, squash outside `Tween`
**Date:** 2026-09-26
**Decision:** Parallax is a CPU position remap applied at extract time: `parallax_world_position(authored, scroll_factor, camera_position) = authored + camera_position * (1 - scroll_factor)`, with `ParallaxLayer { scroll_factor: Vec2 }` in `tungsten-core`. `extract_sprites_default` applies it, and a custom extract calls the same function; `BatchKey`, `z_norm` and `crates/tungsten-render/` are unchanged, and a sprite without `ParallaxLayer` extracts byte-identically to pre-M30. `depth_bucket` is not introduced — `Sprite.z_order` stays the sole ordering authority. Camera shake gains a trauma envelope over the existing sine carrier: `CameraController` carries `shake_trauma` / `shake_decay` / `shake_max_offset` and `shake_offset()` returns `(shake_amplitude + shake_max_offset * trauma²)` modulated by `shake_phase`; `shake_tick_system` drains `EventQueue<ShakeEvent>` into clamped trauma and decays it. Squash/stretch does **not** use `Tween`: `SpriteSquashStretch { on, amount, duration, easing }` plus runtime `SquashStretchState { elapsed, base_scale }` are driven by `squash_stretch_trigger_system` (filters `EventQueue<SquashEvent>` by `SquashTrigger`) and `squash_stretch_tick_system`, which writes `Transform.scale` from the symmetric envelope `sin(easing(t) * π)`. All three systems live in `crates/tungsten/src/game_feel.rs` and are registered by examples, like `camera_update_system`. Both events are registered in `App::new`.
**Why:** `phase4.md` called for one camera matrix per `depth_bucket` through `ExtractSpritesFn`. That seam hands back `Vec<SpriteBatch>` while `stage_render` builds the view-projection separately and `SpritePipeline::update_camera` uploads one camera UBO per frame, so per-bucket matrices would mean a per-batch VP, dynamic-offset camera binding and a changed `render_frame_full` signature — a render-crate rewrite that buys nothing a CPU remap cannot do with one VP. With `depth_bucket` gone as a grouping key, keeping it would only create a second source of ordering truth. `TweenRepeat` has no out-and-back one-shot (`Once` never returns, `PingPong` never completes, `Times(n)` restarts from `from`) and no `Easing` variant is symmetric, so a squash would need a new repeat mode; worse, `D-055` allows one `Tween` per entity and the platformer's player already spends it on the M26 damage flash — the acceptance line needs that same entity to squash on landing and flash on a hit. A dedicated envelope component sidesteps both. `shake_phase` + `shake_frequency_hz` are already a deterministic oscillator, so trauma is an envelope over it and the phase-doc's `shake_noise_seed` is redundant; `core/rng.rs` stays out of the camera path. Trauma adds to `shake_amplitude` rather than replacing it, so `shake_trauma == 0.0` reproduces the pre-M30 offset expression exactly and the M29 render fixtures stay byte-identical.
**Consequences:** Narrows `D-018` and `D-042` (extract may now derive an instance position from a camera resource, not only from `Transform`), and narrows `D-055` (a one-shot scale animation is a component of its own, not a tween; the one-tween rule is untouched). Parallax costs one component lookup per sprite in extract and is not available to tilemap layers — `tilemap_extract.rs` is unchanged, so a parallax backdrop behind a tilemap needs sprite entities and, in `examples/01_platformer/`, an emptied `background` tile layer. Because the remap reads `CameraState.position` after shake is applied, parallax layers inherit the shake at their own scroll factor. All three systems read the current event window only, so an example must register them after the systems that send their events, the trigger before the tick, and `shake_tick_system` before `camera_update_system`. Trigger-before-tick is load-bearing: the tick buffers the terminal removal and `World::flush` applies commands in queue order, so a restart buffered after it would be undone — which is why the trigger restarts an in-flight envelope in place and only buffers an insert when none exists. Arming a fresh envelope therefore costs one frame (`D-039`); restarting an in-flight one is immediate. Trauma is added after `camera_update_system`'s bounds clamp, as `shake_amplitude` always was, so an axis whose view exactly fills the camera bounds has no room to shake into. A custom extract that ignores `Transform.scale` shows no squash — `examples/01_platformer/src/extract.rs` had to read it for the player quad.

## D-074 — Internal release guide and explicit Git commit preflight
**Status:** Superseded by D-079 for final-merged-commit tags and the local squash hand-off; other clauses remain in effect.
**Date:** 2026-09-26
**Decision:** `docs/releases.md` owns release operations, discovered through `tungsten-release`; `tungsten-finalize` owns branch documentation and version cutting. README carries neither release procedures nor version/branch/milestone status. `release.py check/cut` retains the workspace/changelog/DESIGN invariant and stops reading or modifying README. A separate read-only `just release-preflight VERSION --repo OWNER/REPO` inspects committed files, clean/in-progress Git state, live branch/tag refs, explicit GitHub repository and matching release/CI runs. New milestone tags name the final integrated main commit; maintenance/rehearsal branches are explicit. Existing matching tags/releases are resumed or verified without cutting again, published tags stay fixed, rehearsal tags are unique, and retries depend on observed draft/published state. The preflight prints narrowly scoped commands and never executes mutations.
**Why:** The README duplicated internal operations and forced public-facing prose into version tooling. The v0.28.0 release tag and squash-merged main had identical trees but different commit identities; stale local main made the next branch start at the preceding release. Version-string checks alone cannot establish what will ship. Keeping Git/network inspection separate preserves offline and shallow-CI checks.
**Consequences:** Supersedes D-071's README status requirement and handoff location; packaging, tag-triggered builds and version-at-cut rules remain. New skills use D-068's canonical Claude directories and Codex symlinks. Tests use temporary Git repositories and a local bare remote, with GitHub responses mocked. CI stays informational under D-070; no mandatory PR, new runtime dependency, automatic publication/deletion or repository setting change is introduced. Historical tags are preserved.

## D-075 — Physics pair list persists across substeps under a per-proxy travel budget
**Amended by D-080:** the safety-net sweep queries the pair grid or a statics-only grid, with the same result.
**Superseded by D-081:** invalidation rule (a) for a budget trip and the radius formula only; the build, rules (b) and (c), the margin, wake and event clauses stand.
**Date:** 2026-09-27
**Decision:** Pair finding runs once per frame instead of once per substep; the narrow phase, solver, wake test, island union and events stay per substep over that list.

- **Build:** once at frame start (after gather and `sleep_frame_start`), then again only on invalidation.
  - Each proxy gets a pair inflation `r_i`. For an awake dynamic body, `r_i = |v_i|·t_left + ½|g|·t_left² + 2·linear_slop`, where `t_left` is the frame time remaining (the full `dt` at frame start). For statics and sleepers, `r_i = 2·linear_slop`.
  - The grid is restaged at every build from current positions, with each AABB inflated by `r_i`.
  - Each awake initiator queries its own inflated AABB with no half-cell margin: staging is fresh at build, so staleness is zero.
  - A pair is kept when the two inflated AABBs overlap. Initiator rules are unchanged: sleepers never initiate, and awake dynamic pairs appear once.
- **Invalidation, checked before every substep after a build (serial, proxy order):**
  - (a) any awake dynamic proxy breaks `d_i + |v_i|·sub_dt + 2·linear_slop ≤ r_i`, where `d_i` is its accumulated travel since the build;
  - (b) a contact wake happened in the previous substep;
  - (c) the proxy set changed (never mid-frame today).
  - Any of these rebuilds from current state (with the reduced `t_left`) before that substep's narrow phase.
- **Completeness:** a pair missing from the list had AABB gap `> r_a + r_b` at build. At substep k its gap is `≥ gap_build − d_a − d_b > |v_a|·sub_dt + |v_b|·sub_dt + 4·linear_slop ≥` the `D-064` admission margin `|v_a − v_b|·sub_dt + 4·linear_slop`. The AABB gap never exceeds the shape gap, so the exact narrow phase would reject it. Each substep's contact **set** therefore equals the per-substep pair finding's. The narrow phase stays the only admission test.
- **Speculative margins (`D-064`):** unchanged. The per-pair margin is still `|v_a − v_b|·sub_dt + 4·linear_slop`, computed each substep from current velocities; the budget above exists to keep it complete. The static-only sweep safety net keeps its per-substep grid query, which stays valid for statics because they never move and are staged exactly.
- **Wake (`D-065`):** every wake path and threshold is unchanged. Frame-start wakes (external write, despawn, the `wake` API) happen before the first build. A mid-frame contact wake invalidates the list (rule b), so the woken body initiates from the next substep, the same one-substep hop as today. Sleeping–sleeping pairs are still never built. Islands still come from each substep's touching contacts.
- **Collision events (`D-064`/`D-065`/`D-066`):** each substep still emits from its pre-solve narrow phase for `penetration > 0` only, and the frame still drains once. The event **set** is unchanged. The **order** within a substep follows pair-list order, which after a reuse is no longer the order a fresh per-substep query would give. It stays deterministic.

**Why:** Pair finding is about 60% of the awake 3k frame and runs 4× per frame on data that barely moves. `D-067` named pair-list reuse as the next lever after its 25k profile. Building once, and without the 16 px staleness margin in the prefilter, cuts both the query work (about 4× fewer) and the narrow-phase input (about 31k → about 9–12k pairs per substep, estimated from the contact count plus slack). Per-proxy inflation, not a global one, keeps one fast body from fattening every AABB (`D-062`'s rejected full-frame inflation). A fast body simply trips the budget and falls back to rebuilding every substep, today's cost.

**Consequences:**
- **Supersedes in `D-062`:** "staged once at frame start … inflated symmetrically by one substep of travel … reused across substeps under a drift budget … restaged when the total exceeds the half-cell query margin" and "Pair generation … prefilters … query AABB = swept + half-cell margin vs candidate swept AABB". The flat prefix-sum hash, exact-cell entries and serial determinism stand.
- **Supersedes in `D-066`:** "same events, same order" becomes "same events; order is pair-list order".
- **`D-064` and `D-065`:** no clause superseded (margins, wake paths, event gate and island rules re-derived above).
- `D-063`'s per-substep narrow phase stands.
- `D-067` stands: serial, no threads.
- Contact order within a substep can differ from the pre-R5 build, so the Gauss–Seidel results are not bit-identical to it; `physics_determinism.rs` (run-vs-run) still applies.
- New tests:
  - a debug-assert or test harness that compares each substep's contact set against per-substep pair finding on randomized piles, bullets and wakes;
  - a woken-body-over-static-floor test (no fall-through in the waking frame);
  - a budget-trip test for a fast body.
- Benches: `dense_pile_awake/3000` must improve; `pile_plus_bullet` and `projectile_stream` must not regress more than 10%.


## D-076 — Warm-start impulses follow persistent pair indices
**Amended by D-081:** a pair repair carries impulses by pair key through a scratch map, without a keyed-map synchronization.
**Date:** 2026-09-28
**Decision:** While a D-075 pair list is reused, carry each solved normal impulse in a parallel array indexed by the pair's position. The first substep after a pair build seeds admitted contacts from the persistent keyed map; subsequent substeps use the array directly. Clear each pair's cached value before admission, then write back only live solved contacts, so a contact absent for even one substep returns with zero impulse.

Synchronize the keyed map from the immediately preceding substep's live positive impulses before every mid-frame pair rebuild and from the final substep at frame end, including an empty final contact set. Rebuilds can reorder or reverse pairs and introduce contacts after a wake, so they transfer by the existing order-independent entity-generation/tile key rather than by obsolete indices. A pair newly admitted after the first post-build substep starts at zero. Gather still invalidates every frame. No new public API or dependency.

**Why:** After R5, a fresh canonical profile (`perf-runs/20260928T135400Z-physics-stress`) puts impulse-map get/insert at 6.82%/3.44% of CPU samples; awake physics averages 2.67 ms. The common case can remove three map rebuilds and three rounds of contact probes per four-substep frame. A budget trip or wake retains the keyed-map fallback needed for exact transfer.

**Consequences:** Supersedes only D-063's “map is rebuilt from live contacts each substep” clause. Map synchronization normally happens once per frame, with additional synchronization/probes at pair rebuilds. This deliberately narrows R6's original “only once per frame” proposal to preserve the previous-substep semantics on rebuilds. Narrow phase, warm-start scalar values, soft solve, restitution, wake/island rules, events and pair order are unchanged. Regression tests compare every admitted warm start against an independent per-substep map through randomized piles, bullets and wakes, plus disappearance/reappearance and empty-final-substep cases. Deterministic replay, awake throughput and all fast-body benchmark sizes remain required gates.

## D-077 — Symphonia decodes PCM WAV
**Date:** 2026-09-29
**Decision:** Enable symphonia's `pcm` codec feature beside the existing `wav` demuxer, so PCM WAV files decode at load time like OGG, MP3 and AAC. The new transitive crate is `symphonia-codec-pcm`, part of the same MPL-2.0 project. It satisfies D-015 rule 2, because PCM WAV is a well-specified format. No API, mixer or runtime change.
**Why:** `wav` without `pcm` accepted the container but rejected every ordinary WAV with "unsupported codec". `tests/audio_decode.rs` pinned that gap, and a repository review had already recommended closing it. The platformer's generated sound effects are deterministic PCM WAV written by `tools/sfx.py`. Lossy encoding would need an external encoder, and the `--check` bytes would then depend on the encoder version.
**Consequences:** Extends D-028's feature list; nothing is superseded. The decode test now checks the stereo PCM fixture's rate, channels and exact frame count instead of its rejection. `deny.toml` needs no change, because MPL-2.0 is already allowed for symphonia.

## D-078 — Benchmark suite v2
**Date:** 2026-09-30
**Decision:** Six scalable, deterministic benchmarks in one example package replace the four `STRESS_SCENE` scenes of `example-02-sprite-stress` and `scripts/perf-capture.sh`.

- **Benchmarks.** `example-02-bench` runs one benchmark per launch: `physics` (rows `physics` and `physics-sparse`), `ecs`, `churn`, `gpu` (rows `gpu` and `gpu-throughput`), `particles` and `integrated`. Every random choice is seeded and captures pin `dt`, so one build replays an identical workload. Each benchmark carries a `workload_version` that bench-code changes to its work bump; engine changes don't.
- **Scale config.** The binary reads environment variables and owns the knob schema: `TUNGSTEN_BENCH`, then `TUNGSTEN_BENCH_PRESET` (every benchmark has `min` and `default`), `TUNGSTEN_BENCH_SCALE` on the scalable knobs, `TUNGSTEN_BENCH_SET` overrides, then validation, which fails fast on an unknown knob or an out-of-range value. `TUNGSTEN_BENCH_DESCRIBE` prints the schema or the resolved config, and the runner validates every request through it.
- **Ownership.** Each tracked row is judged only on the metrics it owns (`docs/perf/benchmarks.md`): `physics_step` for the physics rows, `update` and its systems for `ecs`, `flush` and the churn systems for `churn`, the render path for the `gpu` rows, the particle stage (as `unattributed`) and `animate_sprites` for `particles`, and `total` p50/p95/p99 plus jitter for `integrated`. Acquire and present pacing are environment, owned by no row.
- **Validity.** A capture is valid when every run exits 0, logs every measured frame, logs the requested `bench-config`, passes the row's guards (no sleeping body in the physics rows, no structural change in `ecs`, a constant population in `churn`, a steady live count in `particles`, the view inside the level in `integrated`) and matches the other runs' determinism digest. An invalid capture stays on disk, the runner exits 3 and compare refuses verdicts unless forced.
- **Tooling.** `scripts/bench.py` (`describe`, `run`, `suite`, `compare`, `capacity`, `baseline`), `scripts/bench_report.py` and `scripts/test-bench.py`, Python 3.12 standard library only, wrapped by `just perf`, replace the bash and awk capture script.
- **Capture defaults (A4).** Captures are telemetry-only; `--profile` opts into perf stat, perf record and a flamegraph. Each benchmark's warm-up plus 300 measured frames; `--repeat 5` for compare-grade captures; GPU timing only in a separate diagnostic run.
- **Verdict rule (A5).** The run is the unit. Δ is the difference of the per-run means, with a 95% Welch interval whose variances are floored at (0.5% of the mean)² and whose t quantiles come from a built-in table. τ = max(τ_rel × baseline, τ_abs): τ_rel is 3% for mean and p50, 5% for p95 and 8% for p99; τ_abs is 0.05 ms for stages and 0.02 ms for systems and GPU passes; peak RSS uses 2% and 2 MiB. `regressed` and `improved` need the interval clear of 0 and |Δ| > τ, `unchanged` needs the interval within ±τ, anything else is `noisy`, and fewer than 3 runs per side gives no verdict. A mismatch in row, `workload_version`, knobs, frames, warm-up, build flags, backend, adapter, present mode, frame latency or machine suppresses every verdict. This replaces "regressions above 10% are noteworthy".
- **Jitter** (p99 − p50 of `total`) takes p99's threshold: τ = max(8% × the baseline's per-run p99 mean, 0.05 ms).
- **Suites.** `just perf suite` captures every tracked row `describe` lists. `--preset P` replaces only the preset of rows whose own preset is `default`, so `physics-sparse` and `gpu-throughput` keep `sparse` and `throughput`; the suite checks before running that every resolved config still measures its row.
- **Capacity search** finds each row's largest scale within a 60 Hz (16.7 ms) or 144 Hz (6.9 ms) p95 budget by bracketing, geometric bisection and three confirmation probes, and reports the limiting stage against the row's declared bottleneck. It is informational, like every perf result (`D-070`).
- **Memory.** Peak RSS comes from `ru_maxrss` through `os.wait4`, with no engine change; RSS growth is sampled from `/proc/<pid>/statm` at 10 Hz.
- **Visual test (A7).** `just visual` compares the `gpu` benchmark's `visual` preset with `examples/02_bench/tests/fixtures/gpu-visual.png`.

**Pacing evidence (A8).** The April 2026 Vulkan matrix, the only evidence for the shipped `Immediate / 1` default, moves here from the old profiling workflow. It was captured on April 16, 2026 on AMD Radeon 660M (`RADV REMBRANDT`) + AMD Ryzen 5 6600H, Arch Linux, `rustc 1.94.1`, with `lto = "thin"`, `codegen-units = 1`, `panic = "abort"` and `target-cpu=native`; `sprite-stress` is the retired 2,000-sprite `baseline` scene:

| Config | Scene | Avg total | p95 total | p99 total | Avg acquire | p95 acquire | p99 acquire |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Immediate / 1` | sprite-stress | `3.74ms` | `13.79ms` | `15.54ms` | `3.39ms` | `13.36ms` | `14.95ms` |
| `Immediate / 2` | sprite-stress | `3.78ms` | `13.95ms` | `16.49ms` | `3.44ms` | `13.35ms` | `15.80ms` |
| `Immediate / 3` | sprite-stress | `3.03ms` | `11.57ms` | `15.31ms` | `2.70ms` | `10.73ms` | `14.93ms` |
| `Mailbox / 2` | sprite-stress | `2.75ms` | `12.05ms` | `15.46ms` | `2.36ms` | `11.20ms` | `15.13ms` |
| `Mailbox / 3` | sprite-stress | `2.46ms` | `11.68ms` | `14.51ms` | `2.07ms` | `10.53ms` | `13.50ms` |
| `Immediate / 1` | platformer | `4.11ms` | `15.00ms` | `16.98ms` | `3.40ms` | `13.73ms` | `15.90ms` |
| `Immediate / 2` | platformer | `4.21ms` | `15.29ms` | `16.77ms` | `3.51ms` | `13.93ms` | `16.13ms` |
| `Mailbox / 2` | platformer | `4.00ms` | `15.50ms` | `16.66ms` | `3.31ms` | `14.66ms` | `15.87ms` |

- `Mailbox / 3` produced the lowest sprite-stress averages; `Mailbox / 2` was close behind and remains a useful explicit pacing-sensitivity knob.
- None of the non-default rows displaced the checked-in default. `Immediate / 1` remains the shipped path because the engine's `auto` mode intentionally preserves the `Immediate`-first no-vsync selection, and the platformer gains were too small to justify a blanket override.
- `display.max_frame_latency = 1` stays the checked-in default; `2` and `3` are opt-in tuning values, not blanket upgrades.
- A GPU spot-check on the same setup (`Immediate / 1`, sprite-stress: average total 3.70 ms, acquire 1.31 ms, scene pass 0.61 ms) showed those captures dominated by presentation pacing, not shader or draw throughput.

**Why:** Each old scene blended several costs: `ecs-high-load` mostly measured a spatial-hash neighbor search, and the `physics-stress` pile fell asleep at frame 286 of 360, so its window mixed two regimes. None scaled along one axis, and the 1,166-line script could not validate configs, read rusage, bisect, compute interval statistics or write HTML. One owner per cost makes a verdict attributable, and guards plus digests turn a plausible-but-wrong capture into an invalid one. Per-run Welch intervals with practical thresholds separate change from noise where a flat 10% rule could not. Jitter's per-run coefficient of variation is about 32%, so a threshold relative to jitter itself sat below the Welch half-width at `--repeat 5` and every A/A would read `noisy`; p99 dominates jitter's noise, so jitter shares its threshold and still catches tail growth when p50 moves the other way. Replacing the preset of `physics-sparse` or `gpu-throughput` would measure another row.

**Consequences:**
- Narrows clause 6 of `D-044`: HUD overhead is measured on the new suite instead of `perf-capture.sh` sprite-stress runs. The rest of `D-044` stands.
- Numbers captured on the old scenes aren't comparable with the suite; baselines restart. Compare and `baseline save` reject captures without `capture.json`.
- The defaults are calibrated to a `total` p95 of 8–16 ms on the reference machine (`docs/perf/benchmarks.md`). `particles` fails its 50% share check by construction, because the default extract dominates it; the owner accepted that calibration.
- Not part of this decision, and not approved: kinematic bodies, sensor colliders, more than 16 lights, GPU particles (G1–G4), particle and tween stage timings (T1), a startup timing line (T2) and allocation counting (M1). Without T1, `particles` owns `unattributed`. RSS growth is reported for every row and judged for none; a `churn` leak threshold is an open proposal.
- Engine findings the suite exposed stay separate work: the burst-emitter latch, tiles at `z_norm` 0 under `gpu_depth`, the per-sprite lit-plus-material warning and text-cache RSS growth.
- CI stays CPU-only (`D-070`): `just script-test` covers the runner; GPU captures, capacity search and the visual test stay local.

## D-079 — Release pull request: agent-run checks, plain Git hand-off, merge publishes
**Date:** 2026-09-30
**Decision:** A milestone release is one hand-off. The agent cuts the version and runs every local check, then ends the task with the exact commands the human pastes: `git add -A`, a one-line `git commit`, an annotated `git tag vX.Y.Z` on that commit, `git push origin 0.NN vX.Y.Z` and `gh pr create` into `main`. `just release-preflight` stays read-only and prints that block: with `--message` it accepts the uncommitted cut as the release commit, and it checks that `main` is contained in the branch. Merging the pull request in GitHub is the approval that publishes. `release.yml` runs on pull requests closed into `main`; when `v<workspace version>` names the merged head commit and the merge commit has that commit's tree, it builds the tagged commit and publishes the GitHub Release. A merged pull request whose version is already released publishes nothing; an unreleased version whose tag does not name the merged head fails the run. Pushed prerelease tags (`vX.Y.Z-<pre>`: rehearsals and release candidates) still publish on push, and `workflow_dispatch` on a tag ref covers maintenance releases and recovery. A tag is fixed once its GitHub Release exists; before that the human may re-point it.
**Why:** `D-074`'s procedure was seven pasted blocks with a local squash merge, refspec pushes, a re-read SHA and a tag created after the merge. On 0.32 the local squash conflicted because the milestone branch was cut from the pre-squash 0.31 tip, and that surfaced mid-release in the human's terminal. Tagging the tested commit and letting GitHub's merge start the build removes the local `main` handling, and the pull request gives one visible approval point with the CI result attached. A tag pushed with a workflow token starts no other workflow, so tagging after the merge would need a second mechanism; tagging before it needs none. Requiring the merge commit's tree to equal the tagged tree keeps `D-074`'s point that version strings alone don't establish what ships.
**Consequences:** Supersedes `D-071`'s tag-push-only trigger and `D-074`'s final-merged-commit tags and local squash hand-off, and restates its "published tags stay fixed" as fixed once released; the rest of both stands, including the read-only preflight, unique rehearsals and state-based recovery. Narrows the "no PR process" clauses of `D-070` and `D-074`: the release pull request is the one the procedure uses, while no branch protection, required check or repository setting is added and CI stays informational. A final tag names the pre-squash milestone commit, so `main`'s release commit has the same tree but a different identity and `git describe` on `main` doesn't see the tag. Pushing a final tag publishes nothing by itself. Every pull request closed into `main` starts a short `release.yml` run that decides whether it is a release. The pull-request trigger can't be rehearsed before it reaches `main`; a prerelease tag rehearses the build and publish jobs, and `gh workflow run release.yml --ref TAG` recovers a merge that started no run. `scripts/release.py version` prints the workspace version for the workflow.

## D-080 — Physics broadphase layout: statics-only sweep grid, direct cell table, compact pair flags
**Date:** 2026-10-01
**Decision:** Four changes to how the broadphase grid is laid out and read. None changes a query result, a candidate order or a benchmark digest.

- **Statics-only sweep grid.** The safety-net sweep (`D-064`) can only hit statics, so it gets a second `SpatialGrid` that holds the static proxies with the `2·linear_slop` inflation a pair build gives them, staged in proxy order. For any query both grids return the same static candidates in the same order, so which one answers is a matter of cost alone. Staging one static costs about as much as one query of the pair grid, so the sweep keeps querying the pair grid until the frame's sweep queries reach the static count, then stages the statics-only grid and uses it for the rest of the frame. Gather invalidates it every frame.
- **Direct cell table.** A build gives every cell of the staged bounds its own slot, row by row, when that table needs at most four slots per staged cell reference, which is about the memory the hashed layout spends on its slots and exact cells. A query then reads the cells of a row as one run of the entry array, with no hash and no exact-cell compare. Larger or sparser bounds keep `D-062`'s hashed table. Both layouts visit cells row by row and a cell's entries in staging order.
- **Inline floor.** Cell coordinates come from a truncating cast corrected for negative non-integers, equal to `floor` then cast for every `f32` (saturation and NaN included), in place of the `floorf` call a generic x86-64 build makes.
- **Pair flags.** The pair query reads "awake dynamic body" from one byte per proxy, filled at the build, not from the 80-byte proxy.

**Why:** The benchmark suite's profiles of 2026-10-01 (`docs/plans/physics-ecs-perf-pass.md`) put the cost in the grid walk, not in the solver. In `physics-sparse` 98.5% of the 8,000 bodies sweep every substep: 31,517 queries per frame, 22% of all samples, against a grid of 8,000 dynamic bodies and 4 walls. The exact-cell compare was 7–13% of samples, `floorf` 2.4–4.0%, and every pair candidate loaded an 80-byte proxy for two flags. All three physics worlds are compact (38 × 62 cells in `physics`, 137 × 79 in `physics-sparse`, about 768 × 48 in `integrated`, against 17,000–36,000 cell references), so the hashed table of 32,768 or 65,536 slots plus 8 bytes of exact cell per reference paid for generality they did not use.

Each part was judged against the one before it with `just perf compare`, five runs a side, on `physics_step` p50:

| Part | `physics` | `physics-sparse` | `integrated` `total` p50 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Statics-only sweep grid | 13.29 → 13.28 ms (`unchanged`) | 8.07 → 6.57 ms | `unchanged` |
| Inline floor | 13.28 → 13.13 ms (`unchanged`) | 6.57 → 6.52 ms (`unchanged`) | `unchanged` |
| Direct cell table | 13.13 → 10.70 ms | 6.52 → 4.91 ms | 12.07 → 10.86 ms |
| Pair flags | 10.70 → 10.04 ms | 4.91 → 4.77 ms (`noisy`) | 10.86 → 10.65 ms (`unchanged`) |

Cells without a verdict read `improved`. Two variants were measured and dropped. Staging the statics-only grid on a frame's first sweep query, without the rent-or-buy rule, moved `integrated`'s `physics_step` p95 from 6.98 to 7.38 ms (`regressed`): it staged 8,528 tile proxies to answer 3.8 queries a frame. Writing the per-entry query body as a closure shared by the two layouts' loops made the compiler emit it out of line, one call per grid entry, and `physics` read 3% slower with the direct table than without it; the body is an `#[inline(always)]` function.

**Consequences:**
- **Amends `D-062`:** "Unbounded cell coordinates map into a power-of-two slot table … each entry stores its exact cell" describes the fall-back layout, and one grid becomes two. The prefix-sum build, lazy build on first query and serial determinism stand.
- **Amends `D-075`:** the note that the sweep "keeps its per-substep grid query" becomes "queries the pair grid or the statics-only grid, with the same result". The pair grid's statics stay exact either way.
- `D-064`'s sweep, its bounding-square promotion and its first-hit rule are unchanged. No new dependency, no `unsafe`, no threads.
- Tests pin what keeps results equal: the statics-only grid against the pair grid's static candidates, in order, over tiles, body-less colliders and sleepers; restaging after the proxy set changes; the rent-or-buy switch; both layouts returning the same ids in the same order on randomized scenes; the four-slots-per-reference threshold and the fall-back; twins of the basic grid tests on the hashed layout; the inline floor against `floor` over every class of `f32`, the cell-edge rules and coordinates beyond `i32`.
- A world with many statics and few fast bodies never stages the second grid; one with more sweep queries than statics pays for staging once per frame.

## D-081 — Physics pair list is repaired for tripped proxies instead of rebuilt
**Date:** 2026-10-01
**Decision:** A proxy that runs out of travel budget gets its own pairs rebuilt; the pair list, the grid and every other proxy's budget stay. `D-075`'s build still runs at frame start, after a contact wake and when the proxy set changes.

- **Budget.** An awake dynamic proxy gets `r_i = |v_i|·t_left + |g|·h²·(n−1)(n+2)/2 + m + 2·linear_slop`, where `h` is the substep, `n = t_left / h` the substeps left and `m` the margin, `2·linear_slop` (0.5 px at the default slop). Statics and sleepers keep `2·linear_slop`. The gravity term is the exact worst case: the integrator adds `g·h` to the velocity before it moves the body, so after `k` substeps a body falling along gravity has travelled `k·|v|·h + g·h²·k(k+1)/2` and moves at `|v| + k·g·h`. The last check of the frame (`k = n − 1`) therefore needs `|v|·t_left + g·h²·(n−1)(n+2)/2`. `D-075`'s `½·g·t_left²` is `g·h²·(n−2)/2` short of that: one `g·h²`, 0.0156 px at 900 px/s² and 240 Hz, in a four-substep frame.
- **Check, before every substep after a build (serial, proxy order).** The tripped set `E` holds every awake dynamic proxy with `d_i + |v_i|·h + 2·linear_slop > r_i` (`D-075`'s test, unchanged).
  - `E` empty: reuse the list.
  - `E` larger than a quarter of the awake dynamic proxies: rebuild, as `D-075` did for any trip.
  - Otherwise repair.
- **Repair.**
  1. Remove every pair with a member in `E`, compacting the pair list and the carried impulses together and keeping their order. Each removed pair's nonzero carried impulse goes into a scratch map under its pair key.
  2. Each member gets a fresh `r_i` and inflated AABB from its current position and velocity and the remaining time; its travel resets to 0.
  3. `E` joins `R`, the proxies repaired since the last build. A second grid is restaged from `R` with their current inflated AABBs, because the main grid still holds the cells they had at the build.
  4. Each member `e`, in index order, queries the main grid, ignoring candidates in `R`, and then the repair grid. A candidate `c` other than `e` whose inflated AABB overlaps `e`'s is paired by this rule, which is not the build's initiator rule:
     - `c` static or sleeping: pair, stored `(e, c)`;
     - `c` awake dynamic and not in `E`, whether or not it is in `R`: pair whatever its index, stored (lower index, higher index), the orientation a build stores;
     - `c` in `E`: pair only when `c > e`, stored `(e, c)`, so a pair between two members is added once.

     A build skips an awake dynamic candidate at or below the querying index because that candidate runs its own query. In a repair only the members of `E` query, so the build's rule would drop every pair between a tripped proxy and an untripped awake body of lower index.
  5. An admitted pair takes its impulse from the scratch map, or zero.
- **Completeness.** `D-075`'s argument holds with radii assigned at different times. Call a proxy's anchor its position at its last build or repair, where its radius and inflated AABB were set and its travel `d` was reset. After a repair the anchored inflated AABBs of a pair that is not in the list do not overlap: nothing changed for a pair without a member of `E`, and step 4 finds every overlap of a member's new AABB (candidates outside `R` through the main grid, whose cells cover their unchanged AABBs, and candidates in `R` through the repair grid). The AABB gap between the anchors therefore exceeds `r_a + r_b`. Each proxy is at most `d` from its anchor, so the gap at any later substep exceeds `(r_a − d_a) + (r_b − d_b) ≥ |v_a|·h + |v_b|·h + 4·linear_slop`, which is at least the `D-064` admission margin. Each substep's contact set still equals per-substep pair finding's.
- **Impulses (`D-076`).** A repair leaves the keyed map and the seed flag alone. A kept pair keeps its slot's value, a re-admitted pair gets its own value back through the scratch map, and a pair that was not in the list, or whose contact was absent in the previous substep, starts at zero. Those are the values the keyed map would give.
- **Pair order.** A repaired list is the kept pairs in their old order followed by the re-admitted ones in member order, which is not the order of a fresh build. Solver order, and so trajectories, differ from `D-075`'s. They stay deterministic: the step is serial and every pass runs in proxy or pair order.

**Why:** Exact call counts on 2026-10-01 (`docs/plans/physics-ecs-perf-pass.md`) showed `D-075`'s list never surviving a substep: 3.99 builds per frame in `physics` and 3.20 in `physics-sparse`, 70% and 54% of all samples. With 8,000 moving bodies one always tripped: every falling body through the short gravity term, half the bodies in a world without gravity because both sides of the check are equal and `f32` rounding decides, and 20–100 bodies a substep whose speed a contact changed. Each trip restaged 8,521 proxies and ran 8,000 queries. A margin alone does not fix it: under `D-075`'s rule the margin must reach 8 px before `physics` builds once per frame, and the list then holds 98,958 pairs. Re-pairing only the offenders is what Box2D does with fattened AABBs and its move buffer.

The margin was swept on the sum of the two rows' `physics_step` p50: 10.46 ms at 0, 9.55 ms at `2·linear_slop` and 9.67 ms at `4·linear_slop`. Without a margin the rounding case still trips more than a quarter of the sparse bodies and falls back to a rebuild. With `2·linear_slop` a frame is one build plus 3.0 repairs of 153 proxies each in `physics`, and one build plus 2.1 repairs of 164 in `physics-sparse`; the fall-back never fires in either.

Against the build before it (`just perf compare`, five runs a side), `physics_step` p50 went from 10.04 to 5.86 ms in `physics` and from 4.77 to 3.67 ms in `physics-sparse`, whose p95 fell from 5.84 to 3.75 ms because the four-build frames are gone. `integrated` did not move (`total` p50 10.65 → 10.75 ms, `unchanged`): 1,003 of its 1,403 builds in 400 frames follow a contact wake, which this decision keeps as a rebuild.

**Consequences:**
- **Supersedes in `D-075`:** invalidation rule (a) for a budget trip ("Any of these rebuilds from current state") and the radius `|v_i|·t_left + ½|g|·t_left² + 2·linear_slop`. The build itself, rules (b) and (c), the speculative margins, the wake clause and the event clause stand.
- **Amends `D-076`:** a repair carries impulses by pair key through a scratch map, without a keyed-map synchronization. The synchronization before a rebuild and at frame end stands, and so does the rule that a contact absent for one substep returns with zero impulse.
- **Digests.** Pair order changes trajectories, so the three physics-bearing benchmark digests changed: `physics` `c972b2818d486617` → `86ffcabcdb15eed1`, `physics-sparse` `c95fadc938a3689e` → `5899f9c8a69d79b1`, `integrated` `9b2617e4c1ab23f7` → `5f031f4d947964cb`. `workload_version` stays 1, because the benchmarks' work did not change (`D-078`).
- **`physics.pairs`.** The list is built once for the whole frame's travel plus the margin, not rebuilt for the remainder at every substep, so the final-substep count rises from 15,150 to 36,144 in `physics` and from 1,831 to 15,298 in `physics-sparse` (6,677 to 7,034 in `integrated`). Compare flags that as workload drift against older captures. Contacts per substep are unchanged (8,861 and 8,846 in `physics`).
- **`D-075`'s bench gate.** `projectile_stream` is 41–57% faster at 3,000, 10,000 and 25,000 bodies; `pile_plus_bullet` reads no change at 10,000 and 21% faster at 25,000.
